## Claude

I'll start by mapping out the live environment — reading the actual config files and checking installed versions — before researching best practices for each tool.

I've read every relevant config file directly and cross-checked findings against official docs (Starship, tmux, Yazi, ncdu's own man page) and current community practice for the newer AI-agent-specific angles (tmux). Here's the full picture.

---

## Starship

**What it is:** the thing that draws your shell prompt — instead of a plain `user@host:~/dir$`, it shows contextual info like your git branch and language versions, and it's the same tool across bash/zsh/fish.

**Current state** (`~/.config/starship.toml`, verified on disk, 1.26.0 installed): a powerline-style prompt built from `$os → $directory → $git_branch → $git_status → $nodejs/$bun/$rust/$golang/$php → $time → $character`. `git_status` does include `$ahead_behind`, which is good. The `time` module shows `%R` (HH:MM) in the most prominent right-hand segment. There is **no `cmd_duration` module and no `status`/exit-code module anywhere in the file**.

**Researched best practice** ([starship.rs/config](https://starship.rs/config/)): `cmd_duration` defaults to a 2000ms threshold (`min_time`) — it only appears for slow commands, so it costs nothing when idle. `status` is disabled by default and must be turned on explicitly (`disabled = false`) to show the last exit code. For remote/container awareness there's `hostname` (`ssh_only = true` by default — only shows over SSH, which is exactly right for a local WSL box), `username`, and `container`. There's no dedicated "AI agent session" module — that's a genuine gap in Starship itself, not something you're missing in config.

- **Crucial** — no exit-code indicator. Confidence: **high**. Verified both by reading the file (no `[status]` table, `character` untouched) and by checking docs (status module is off by default, so it's not "on but invisible"). For someone running shells all day where linters/builds/agent-invoked commands fail silently, this is a real blind spot, not a style preference.
- **Crucial** — no `cmd_duration`. Confidence: **high**, same reasoning — verified absent, and it's a zero-cost addition (only renders past the threshold).
- **Crucial** — `$time` occupies the most prominent slot showing only clock time, which is redundant: tmux's status-right (see below) *already* shows `%H:%M %d-%b-%y` via tmux-continuum's auto-injected string. Confidence: **medium** — I've verified the duplication as fact; whether to *replace* vs. supplement that slot is a judgment call, so I'm not calling the module itself wrong, just poorly prioritized real estate given you already see the clock elsewhere.
- **Nice-to-have**: `shlvl` (catches accidentally-nested shells, common when agents shell out), `jobs` (background job count). Confidence: medium — genuinely useful, not something you're missing acutely.

---

## tmux

**What it is:** a terminal multiplexer — it keeps multiple panes/windows running inside one persistent session, so an agent process (or SSH connection) survives you closing the terminal window, and lets you tile several things on screen at once.

**Current state** (`~/.tmux.conf`, verified — 6 lines total; tmux 3.4): `mouse on`, plus `tpm` + `tmux-resurrect` + `tmux-continuum` with `@continuum-restore on`. Everything else is compiled-in default, which I inspected directly by spinning up an isolated throwaway tmux socket (no effect on your real sessions): `status-left` is just `[session_name]`, `status-right` is continuum's autosave string + pane title + clock, `base-index = 0`, `prefix = C-b`, and `pane-border-status` is unset (no per-pane labels).

**Researched best practice**: `pane-border-status top` is a **native tmux 3.2+ feature, no plugin required** — it prints a label above each pane, which is exactly what you'd want with several agent panes side by side so you can tell at a glance which repo/agent is in which pane. `base-index 1` / `pane-base-index 1` is standard ergonomic advice (window 1 sits under the `1` key instead of `0` sitting under `0`, which is a keyboard-layout mismatch). Genuinely new since your training-relevant window: there's now a small ecosystem of 2026 tmux plugins built *specifically* for "multiple AI coding agents in tmux panes" — `tmux-agent-indicator`, `tmux-agent-status`, `tmux-agent-sidebar`, `tmux-agent-monitor`, `tmux-agent-tamagotchi` — they hook into Claude Code/Codex lifecycle events to color pane borders or the status line by agent state (running / needs-input / done) and offer an fzf-style jump to whichever pane needs you. These are all small, young repos — I can't vouch for maintenance quality, just that the category now exists and matches your stated use case closely.

- **Crucial**: no `pane-border-status` — with zero plugins, several agent panes open side by side are visually indistinguishable except by clicking in. This directly blocks the parallel-agent-sessions workflow you named. Confidence: **high** (verified absence; it's a built-in option, not a plugin gap).
- **Nice-to-have**: `base-index 1`/`pane-base-index 1` (confidence: high it works, medium it matters — pure ergonomics); trying one of the agent-status plugins above once you vet its maintenance state (confidence: medium — real and on-point, but unverified quality); `tmux-fzf` for fast session/window switching once you have many repos open at once.

---

## Yazi

**What it is:** a terminal file manager (like `ranger`/`nnn`, but Rust and fast) that shows live previews of files — text, images, archives — as you navigate directories with the keyboard.

**Current state**: `~/.config/yazi/` **does not exist at all** — confirmed, not just unreadable. You're on 100% built-in defaults, version 26.9.1. Supporting binaries: `ripgrep` and `fd` are present (Yazi uses these for search/filter), `fzf` is present; `ueberzugpp`, `chafa`, and `ffmpegthumbnailer` are **absent**.

**Researched best practice** ([yazi-rs.github.io](https://yazi-rs.github.io/docs/image-preview/)): config splits into `yazi.toml` (behavior/preview), `keymap.toml`, `theme.toml`. Image preview auto-detects a backend by reading `$TERM`, `$TERM_PROGRAM`, and `$XDG_SESSION_TYPE`, in priority order: kitty protocol → iTerm2 protocol → Sixel → Überzug++ (X11/Wayland) → Chafa (ASCII fallback, needs Chafa ≥1.16.0).

- **Crucial**: I checked your actual Windows Terminal version from inside WSL — **1.24.11911.0**, which is above the 1.22.10352.0 threshold where Windows Terminal gained Sixel support. But `$TERM_PROGRAM` and `$XDG_SESSION_TYPE` are both **empty** in this shell — those are exactly the signals Yazi's auto-detection reads, and Windows Terminal is known not to set `$TERM_PROGRAM` into the WSL session. With no Überzug++/Chafa fallback installed either, image previews are very likely rendering blank right now, silently. Confidence: **medium** — I verified every input to this chain (version, env vars, missing fallback binaries) but haven't visually confirmed a blank preview pane, since that needs an interactive TUI session I can't drive here.
- **Nice-to-have**: once you do build a config, `yazi-rs/plugins:git` (git status shown inline per file — directly useful across many repos) and `full-border.yazi` are the two most commonly recommended community plugins; `smart-filter.yazi` for faster fuzzy-filtering in large trees.

---

## ncdu

**What it is:** an interactive, keyboard-driven disk-usage browser — point it at a directory and it shows what's eating space, letting you drill down, much faster to use than reading raw `du` output.

**Current state**: v1.19 installed (the C/"LTS" line — current LTS is 1.22, and there's a separate Zig-based 2.x rewrite at 2.9.2 with parallel scanning; you're a couple patches behind the LTS line but not meaningfully outdated). **No config file exists.** I read the actual installed man page directly (not a version-agnostic web page) and confirmed config-file support (`~/.config/ncdu/config`, one CLI flag per line) is already present in this 1.x build — it's not a 2.x-only feature.

- **Crucial**: none. It works correctly as installed.
- **Nice-to-have**: a config with `-e` (extended info), `--color dark`, `--exclude .git`, `--exclude node_modules`, `--confirm-quit` would remove retyping flags every run — genuinely handy for a JS-heavy dev tree, but purely a convenience, nothing is currently wrong. Confidence: high that it works, medium that it's worth the effort.

---

## semgrep

**What it is:** a static-analysis linter for security and correctness patterns in source code — think "grep with actual language understanding and a security rule library."

**Current state**: v1.177.0 installed. `~/.semgrep/settings.yml` contains only a telemetry-opt-out flag and an anonymous ID — not logged in, no ruleset pinned. There's no project to check `.semgrep.yml`/`.semgrepignore` against from the home directory (those are per-repo, not global).

**Researched best practice**: `semgrep scan --config auto` and registry packs (`p/security-audit`, `p/ci`) run **fully locally, no login required**. `semgrep ci` is the one that requires a `SEMGREP_APP_TOKEN` and talks to the Semgrep AppSec Platform. The most-cited real-world failure mode (Trail of Bits and community writeups) is config drift: a pre-commit hook pinned to a narrow ruleset like `p/security-audit` while CI runs `--config auto` produces "local green, CI red" — the fix is making local and CI use the same ruleset. For `.semgrepignore`, the current convention is `:include .gitignore` so you don't duplicate patterns you already maintain in git.

- **Crucial**: none — semgrep config is inherently per-project, and there's nothing globally misconfigured in this home-directory scan.
- **Nice-to-have**: when you set semgrep up inside an actual repo, use `.semgrepignore` with `:include .gitignore`, and deliberately match whatever ruleset CI runs in any local/pre-commit invocation to avoid the drift problem above. Confidence: medium-high — well-documented pattern, but contingent on how you eventually wire CI.

---

## Base toolchain (git/gh, node, pnpm, uv)

Only git's output is worth flagging here — gh/node/pnpm/uv defaults are already fine for this use case.

**Current state**: git 2.43.0, `core.pager` unset (defaults to plain `less`), no diff highlighter configured. `bat` is installed but not wired into git.

- **Crucial**: none — plain `git diff` works.
- **Nice-to-have**: `git-delta` (not installed) as `core.pager`, or a `bat`-based wrapper, gives syntax-highlighted, side-by-side diffs — genuinely easier to review when you're reading a lot of agent-generated diffs all day. Confidence: medium — real quality-of-life gain, but it's a new package install, not a config fix I can demonstrate as a diff to an existing file.

---

**What would even more elevate your WSL experience?** One gap kept surfacing that didn't fit neatly under any single tool above: the new tmux "agent status" plugins solve visibility *while you're looking at the terminal*, but none of them push a notification *out* to Windows when you're not — e.g., a toast or sound when an agent finishes or needs input while you've alt-tabbed away. Is that kind of cross-session, outside-the-terminal notification something you'd want wired up, or is staying inside tmux/the terminal enough for how you work?

---
       --ignore-config
           Do not attempt to load any configuration files.

   Scan Options
       These  options  affect  the  scanning progress, and have no effect when
       importing directory information from a file.

       -x, --one-file-system
           Do not cross filesystem  boundaries,  i.e.  only  count  files  and
           directories on the same filesystem as the directory being scanned.

       --cross-file-system
           Do  cross  filesystem  boundaries.  This is the default, but can be
           specified to overrule a previously given "-x".

       --exclude PATTERN
           Exclude files that match PATTERN. The files will still be displayed
           by default, but are not counted towards the disk usage  statistics.
           This argument can be added multiple times to add more patterns.

       -X FILE, --exclude-from FILE
           Exclude  files  that  match any pattern in FILE. Patterns should be
--
CONFIGURATION
       Ncdu   can   be   configured   by   placing   command-line  options  in
       "/etc/ncdu.conf" or "$HOME/.config/ncdu/config". If both  files  exist,
       the  system configuration will be loaded before the user configuration,
       allowing users to override options set  in  the  system  configuration.
       Options  given  on  the  command  line will override options set in the
       configuration  files.  The  files  will  not  be  read  at   all   when
       "--ignore-config" is given on the command line.

       The  configuration  file  format  is simply one command line option per
       line. Lines starting with "#" are ignored. Example configuration file:

         # Always enable extended mode
         -e

         # Disable file deletion
         --disable-delete

         # Exclude .git directories
         --exclude .git

KEYS
       ?   Show help + keys + about screen

       up, down, j, k

           Open selected directory

       left, <, h
--

<apply>
**Starship** (~/.config/starship.toml):
- Add a [status] table with disabled = false, so failed commands actually
  show a visible exit-code indicator - verified currently absent.
- Add [cmd_duration] (default 2000ms min_time is correct - it only shows
  for slow commands, costs nothing when idle).
- The $time module currently occupies the most prominent right-hand slot
  and only shows clock time - tmux's own status bar already shows the
  clock via tmux-continuum. Move $time to a less prominent position or
  replace it with something not already visible elsewhere (e.g. $shlvl or
  $jobs), rather than showing the same clock twice.
- Add $shlvl (catches accidentally-nested shells) and $jobs (background
  job count).

**tmux** (~/.tmux.conf):
- Add `pane-border-status top` - this is a built-in tmux 3.2+ feature, no
  plugin needed. With several agent panes open side by side, this is the
  fix for "which pane is which repo" - currently there is no way to tell
  panes apart without clicking into each one.
- Set `base-index 1` and `pane-base-index 1` (window/pane 1 sits under the
  1 key, matching normal keyboard layout instead of 0).
- Rebind the prefix from the tmux default (Ctrl-b) to Ctrl-a, and rebind
  the default split keys (%, ") to | (horizontal) and - (vertical) -
  this matches WezTerm's own default leader and split keys, confirmed
  directly against https://wezterm.org/config/default-keys.html, and is
  also the independently-converged-on convention for modernizing tmux.
  Unbind the old % / " bindings so they don't linger as a second way to
  do the same thing.
- Install win32yank (a small prebuilt binary from
  https://github.com/equalsraf/win32yank/releases - WSL has no shared
  graphical clipboard with Windows by default) and bind it in copy-mode:
  `bind -T copy-mode-vi y send-keys -X copy-pipe-and-cancel "win32yank.exe -i"`.
  Verify by copying text in a tmux pane and pasting it into a Windows app.
- Install the tmux-fzf plugin via TPM for fast session/window switching
  across many repos.
- Look at the current state of the small 2026 "AI agent status for tmux"
  plugin category (tmux-agent-indicator, tmux-agent-status,
  tmux-agent-sidebar, tmux-agent-monitor were named as existing but
  unvetted) - check each one's actual maintenance activity (recent
  commits, open issues, star count) before installing any of them. If
  none look genuinely maintained, skip this one and say so rather than
  installing something fragile.

**Yazi** (~/.config/yazi/ - does not exist yet, build it from scratch):
- Create yazi.toml, keymap.toml, theme.toml.
- Image previews are very likely broken right now: Windows Terminal here
  is new enough for Sixel support, but $TERM_PROGRAM and $XDG_SESSION_TYPE
  are both empty in this shell, which is exactly what Yazi's backend
  auto-detection reads, and no Überzug++/Chafa fallback is installed
  either. Install chafa (apt) as the fallback backend, and verify
  image preview actually renders on a real image file in one of the
  project directories - don't just assume the config is correct, look at
  the actual pane.
- Add the yazi-rs/plugins:git plugin (inline git status per file - useful
  across many repos), full-border.yazi, and smart-filter.yazi.

**ncdu** (~/.config/ncdu/config - does not exist yet):
- Create it with: -e (extended info), --color dark, --exclude .git,
  --exclude node_modules, --confirm-quit.

**semgrep**: no global config change - confirmed nothing is globally
misconfigured. When you do set it up inside a real repo later, use
.semgrepignore with `:include .gitignore`, and make sure whatever ruleset
runs locally/pre-commit matches whatever CI eventually runs, to avoid the

- `Write` — `/home/anant_gupta/.tmux.conf`
  ```
set -g mouse on

# Keyboard-matching indices: window/pane 1 sits under the 1 key
set -g base-index 1
setw -g pane-base-index 1

# Vi-style copy-mode keys (needed for the copy-mode-vi binding below to fire)
setw -g mode-keys vi

# Per-pane labels so several agent panes side by side are distinguishable
set -g pane-border-status top
set -g pane-border-format "#{pane_index} #{b:pane_current_path}"

# Prefix: Ctrl-a, matching WezTerm's own default leader
unbind C-b
set -g prefix C-a
bind C-a send-prefix

# Splits: | and -, matching WezTerm's default split keys
unbind %
unbind '"'
bind | split-window -h
bind - split-window -v

# Clipboard bridge to Windows (WSL has no shared GUI clipboard by default)
bind -T copy-mode-vi y send-keys -X copy-pipe-and-cancel "win32yank.exe -i"

set -g @plugin 'tmux-plugins/tpm'
set -g @plugin 'tmux-plugins/tmux-resurrect'
set -g @plugin 'tmux-plugins/tmux-continuum'
set -g @continuum-restore 'on'
set -g @plugin 'sainnhe/tmux-fzf'
set -g @plugin 'samleeney/tmux-agent-status'

run '~/.tmux/plugins/tpm/tpm'

---
#!/usr/bin/env bash

CURRENT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"

# Default key bindings
default_switcher_key="S"
default_next_done_key="N"
default_wait_key="W"
default_park_key="p"

# Get user configuration or use defaults.
switcher_key=$(tmux show-option -gqv "@agent-status-key")
next_done_key=$(tmux show-option -gqv "@agent-next-done-key")
wait_key=$(tmux show-option -gqv "@agent-wait-key")
park_key=$(tmux show-option -gqv "@agent-park-key")

[ -z "$switcher_key" ] && switcher_key="$default_switcher_key"
[ -z "$next_done_key" ] && next_done_key="$default_next_done_key"
[ -z "$wait_key" ] && wait_key="$default_wait_key"
[ -z "$park_key" ] && park_key="$default_park_key"

# Default switcher view: "tree" (hierarchical session/window/pane, default)
# or "agents" (flat list of every agent pane). Toggle mid-session with ctrl-f.
switcher_default_mode=$(tmux show-option -gqv "@agent-switcher-default-mode")
case "$switcher_default_mode" in
    tree|agents) ;;
    *) switcher_default_mode="tree" ;;
esac

# Switcher style: "popup" (fzf only), "sidebar" (sidebar only), or "both" (default)
switcher_style=$(tmux show-option -gqv "@agent-switcher-style")
[ -z "$switcher_style" ] && switcher_style="both"

# Display method: "popup" (default, requires tmux 3.2+) or "window" (fallback)
display_method=$(tmux show-option -gqv "@agent-status-display-method")
[ -z "$display_method" ] && display_method="popup"

# Sidebar key (used in "both" mode; in "sidebar" mode the main switcher key is used)
sidebar_key=$(tmux show-option -gqv "@agent-sidebar-key")
[ -z "$sidebar_key" ] && sidebar_key="o"

# Helper to bind the fzf switcher using the configured display method.
# Passes the default mode through via TMUX_AGENT_SWITCHER_MODE so the
# script can pick the right initial view without an extra CLI flag.
bind_fzf_switcher() {
    local key="$1"
    local launch
    case "$display_method" in
        "window")
            printf -v launch 'env TMUX_AGENT_SWITCHER_MODE=%q %q' \
                "$switcher_default_mode" "$CURRENT_DIR/scripts/hook-based-switcher.sh"
            tmux bind-key "$key" new-window -n "agent-status" "$launch"
            ;;
        "popup"|*)
            # Popup geometry varies by mode + preview state, so the popup
            # loop wrapper owns the display-popup invocation and relaunches
            # with new dimensions when the inner script requests it.
            printf -v launch 'env TMUX_AGENT_SWITCHER_MODE=%q %q' \
                "$switcher_default_mode" "$CURRENT_DIR/scripts/switcher-popup-loop.sh"
            tmux bind-key "$key" run-shell -b "$launch"
  ```

42-
43-```bash
44-brew install bash
45-```
46-
47:The plugin auto-detects Homebrew Bash at `/opt/homebrew/bin/bash` or `/usr/local/bin/bash` when macOS launches scripts with the system Bash 3.2.
48-If Bash is installed somewhere else, set `TMUX_AGENT_STATUS_BASH` to that path.
49-
50-The collector uses `flock`, or Perl when `flock` is unavailable, to prevent
51-duplicate daemons. Install either one if neither is available on your system.
52-
--
249-
250-Run `/hooks` in the CLI to confirm the command hooks are loaded, and trust them if Devin marks them as pending review.
251-
252-Devin state is hook-based. The handler marks the session or pane `working` on `UserPromptSubmit`/`PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse`, resets it to `done` on `Stop`, and seeds resumed sessions on `SessionStart`.
253-
254:Without hooks, the collector still auto-detects a running `devin` process inside a pane (presence only); hooks are required for live `working`/`done` state.
255-
256-## Custom Agent Integration
257-
258-Integrate any AI coding tool with either of these approaches:
259-
260-1. Write `working`, `done`, or `wait` to `~/.cache/tmux-agent-status/<session>.status`
261-2. For pane-level parking or per-pane state, write to `~/.cache/tmux-agent-status/panes/<session>_<pane>.status` and `~/.cache/tmux-agent-status/parked/<session>_<pane>.parked`
262:3. Extend the collector scan in [`scripts/lib/collect.sh`](scripts/lib/collect.sh) if you want automatic process-based tracking
263-
264-## Usage
265-

  ```bash
MARKER="TMUX_COPY_TEST_$(date +%s)"
tmux send-keys -t work.1 "clear; printf '%s\n' $MARKER" Enter
sleep 0.3
tmux copy-mode -t work.1
sleep 0.2
tmux send-keys -t work.1 -X history-top
sleep 0.1
tmux send-keys -t work.1 -X search-forward "$MARKER"
sleep 0.2
tmux send-keys -t work.1 V
sleep 0.1
tmux send-keys -t work.1 y
sleep 0.3
READBACK=$(~/.local/bin/win32yank.exe -o)
echo "marker sent   : $MARKER"
echo "clipboard now : $READBACK"
echo "$READBACK" | grep -q "$MARKER" && echo "TMUX COPY-MODE BINDING: WORKS (real Windows clipboard round-trip)" || echo "TMUX COPY-MODE BINDING: FAILED"
  ```

=== fzf-related bindings ===
bind-key    -T prefix       F                      run-shell -b "TMUX_FZF_CLIENT='#{client_tty}' /home/anant_gupta/.tmux/plugins/tmux-fzf/main.sh"

=== agent-status bindings (S/N/W/p/o) ===
bind-key    -T prefix       N                      run-shell /home/anant_gupta/.tmux/plugins/tmux-agent-status/scripts/next-done-project.sh
bind-key    -T prefix       S                      run-shell -b "env TMUX_AGENT_SWITCHER_MODE=tree /home/anant_gupta/.tmux/plugins/tmux-agent-status/scripts/switcher-popup-loop.sh"
bind-key    -T prefix       W                      run-shell /home/anant_gupta/.tmux/plugins/tmux-agent-status/scripts/wait-session.sh
bind-key    -T prefix       o                      run-shell /home/anant_gupta/.tmux/plugins/tmux-agent-status/scripts/sidebar-toggle.sh
bind-key    -T prefix       p                      run-shell /home/anant_gupta/.tmux/plugins/tmux-agent-status/scripts/park-session.sh

=== copy-mode-vi y binding registered ===
bind-key -T copy-mode-vi C-y               send-keys -X scroll-up
bind-key -T copy-mode-vi y                 send-keys -X copy-pipe-and-cancel "win32yank.exe -i"

=== split bindings ===
bind-key    -T prefix       -                      split-window -v
bind-key    -T prefix       >                      display-menu -T "#[align=centre]#{pane_index} (#{pane_id})" -x P -y P "#{?#{m/r:(copy|view)-mode,#{pane_mode}},Go To Top,}" < { send-keys -X history-top } "#{?#{m/r:(copy|view)-mode,#{pane_mode}},Go To Bottom,}" > { send-keys -X history-bottom } '' "#{?mouse_word,Search For #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_word},}" C-r { if-shell -F "#{?#{m/r:(copy|view)-mode,#{pane_mode}},0,1}" "copy-mode -t=" ; send-keys -X -t = search-backward "#{q:mouse_word}" } "#{?mouse_word,Type #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_word},}" C-y { copy-mode -q ; send-keys -l "#{q:mouse_word}" } "#{?mouse_word,Copy #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_word},}" c { copy-mode -q ; set-buffer "#{q:mouse_word}" } "#{?mouse_line,Copy Line,}" l { copy-mode -q ; set-buffer "#{q:mouse_line}" } '' "#{?mouse_hyperlink,Type #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_hyperlink},}" C-h { copy-mode -q ; send-keys -l "#{q:mouse_hyperlink}" } "#{?mouse_hyperlink,Copy #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_hyperlink},}" h { copy-mode -q ; set-buffer "#{q:mouse_hyperlink}" } '' "Horizontal Split" h { split-window -h } "Vertical Split" v { split-window -v } '' "#{?#{>:#{window_panes},1},,-}Swap Up" u { swap-pane -U } "#{?#{>:#{window_panes},1},,-}Swap Down" d { swap-pane -D } "#{?pane_marked_set,,-}Swap Marked" s { swap-pane } '' Kill X { kill-pane } Respawn R { respawn-pane -k } "#{?pane_marked,Unmark,Mark}" m { select-pane -m } "#{?#{>:#{window_panes},1},,-}#{?window_zoomed_flag,Unzoom,Zoom}" z { resize-pane -Z }
bind-key    -T prefix       |                      split-window -h
bind-key    -T root         MouseDown3Pane         if-shell -F -t = "#{||:#{mouse_any_flag},#{&&:#{pane_in_mode},#{?#{m/r:(copy|view)-mode,#{pane_mode}},0,1}}}" { select-pane -t = ; send-keys -M } { display-menu -T "#[align=centre]#{pane_index} (#{pane_id})" -t = -x M -y M "#{?#{m/r:(copy|view)-mode,#{pane_mode}},Go To Top,}" < { send-keys -X history-top } "#{?#{m/r:(copy|view)-mode,#{pane_mode}},Go To Bottom,}" > { send-keys -X history-bottom } '' "#{?mouse_word,Search For #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_word},}" C-r { if-shell -F "#{?#{m/r:(copy|view)-mode,#{pane_mode}},0,1}" "copy-mode -t=" ; send-keys -X -t = search-backward "#{q:mouse_word}" } "#{?mouse_word,Type #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_word},}" C-y { copy-mode -q ; send-keys -l "#{q:mouse_word}" } "#{?mouse_word,Copy #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_word},}" c { copy-mode -q ; set-buffer "#{q:mouse_word}" } "#{?mouse_line,Copy Line,}" l { copy-mode -q ; set-buffer "#{q:mouse_line}" } '' "#{?mouse_hyperlink,Type #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_hyperlink},}" C-h { copy-mode -q ; send-keys -l "#{q:mouse_hyperlink}" } "#{?mouse_hyperlink,Copy #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_hyperlink},}" h { copy-mode -q ; set-buffer "#{q:mouse_hyperlink}" } '' "Horizontal Split" h { split-window -h } "Vertical Split" v { split-window -v } '' "#{?#{>:#{window_panes},1},,-}Swap Up" u { swap-pane -U } "#{?#{>:#{window_panes},1},,-}Swap Down" d { swap-pane -D } "#{?pane_marked_set,,-}Swap Marked" s { swap-pane } '' Kill X { kill-pane } Respawn R { respawn-pane -k } "#{?pane_marked,Unmark,Mark}" m { select-pane -m } "#{?#{>:#{window_panes},1},,-}#{?window_zoomed_flag,Unzoom,Zoom}" z { resize-pane -Z } }
bind-key    -T root         M-MouseDown3Pane       display-menu -T "#[align=centre]#{pane_index} (#{pane_id})" -t = -x M -y M "#{?#{m/r:(copy|view)-mode,#{pane_mode}},Go To Top,}" < { send-keys -X history-top } "#{?#{m/r:(copy|view)-mode,#{pane_mode}},Go To Bottom,}" > { send-keys -X history-bottom } '' "#{?mouse_word,Search For #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_word},}" C-r { if-shell -F "#{?#{m/r:(copy|view)-mode,#{pane_mode}},0,1}" "copy-mode -t=" ; send-keys -X -t = search-backward "#{q:mouse_word}" } "#{?mouse_word,Type #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_word},}" C-y { copy-mode -q ; send-keys -l "#{q:mouse_word}" } "#{?mouse_word,Copy #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_word},}" c { copy-mode -q ; set-buffer "#{q:mouse_word}" } "#{?mouse_line,Copy Line,}" l { copy-mode -q ; set-buffer "#{q:mouse_line}" } '' "#{?mouse_hyperlink,Type #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_hyperlink},}" C-h { copy-mode -q ; send-keys -l "#{q:mouse_hyperlink}" } "#{?mouse_hyperlink,Copy #[underscore]#{=/9/...:mouse_hyperlink},}" h { copy-mode -q ; set-buffer "#{q:mouse_hyperlink}" } '' "Horizontal Split" h { split-window -h } "Vertical Split" v { split-window -v } '' "#{?#{>:#{window_panes},1},,-}Swap Up" u { swap-pane -U } "#{?#{>:#{window_panes},1},,-}Swap Down" d { swap-pane -D } "#{?pane_marked_set,,-}Swap Marked" s { swap-pane } '' Kill X { kill-pane } Respawn R { respawn-pane -k } "#{?pane_marked,Unmark,Mark}" m { select-pane -m } "#{?#{>:#{window_panes},1},,-}#{?window_zoomed_flag,Unzoom,Zoom}" z { resize-pane -Z }

rm -rf lazygit_install && mkdir lazygit_install && cd lazygit_install
curl -sL -o lg.tar.gz https://github.com/jesseduffield/lazygit/releases/download/v0.65.1/lazygit_0.65.1_linux_x86_64.tar.gz
tar -xzf lg.tar.gz lazygit
chmod +x lazygit
mv lazygit ~/.local/bin/lazygit
cd /
rm -rf /tmp/lazygit_install
hash -r
lazygit --version
  ```

      name: "delta"
      colorArg: "always"

  autoRefresh: true

gui:

- `Bash` — `sed -n '190,230p' ~/.config/yazi/plugins/git.yazi/main.lua`
  ```
---@param st State
---@param opts Options
local function setup(st, opts)
	st.dirs = {}
	st.repos = {}

	opts = opts or {}
	opts.order = opts.order or 1500

	local styles, signs = theme()
	ps.sub("theme", function()
		styles, signs = theme()
	end)

	Linemode:children_add(function(self)
		if not self._file.in_current then
			return ""
		end

		local url = self._file.url
		local repo = st.dirs[tostring(url.base or url.parent)]
		local code = CODES.unknown
		if repo then
			code = repo == CODES.excluded and CODES.ignored or st.repos[repo][tostring(url):sub(#repo + 2)] or CODES.clean
		end

		if signs[code] == "" then
			return ""
		elseif self._file.is_hovered then
			return ui.Line { " ", signs[code] }
		else
			return ui.Line { " ", ui.Span(signs[code]):style(styles[code]) }
		end
	end, opts.order)
end

---@type UnstableFetcher
local function fetch(_, job)
	local cwd = job.files[1].url.base or job.files[1].url.parent
	local repo = root(cwd)
  ```

curl -fsSL -o /tmp/sesh.json https://api.github.com/repos/joshmedeski/sesh/releases/latest

tar -xf /tmp/sesh.tar.gz -C /tmp sesh
install /tmp/sesh ~/.local/bin/sesh
rm -f /tmp/sesh.json /tmp/sesh.tar.gz /tmp/sesh
~~~
(If that exact asset name 404s, check the release page's real asset list
first - don't guess a second filename blind.)

~~~bash
curl --proto '=https' --tlsv1.2 -sSf https://setup.atuin.sh | bash
~~~

~~~bash
gh extension install dlvhdr/gh-dash
~~~

Add to the end of the existing `JARVIS_WSL_ENV_BLOCK` in `~/.bashrc` (keep
the marker-guard pattern already used there - check for duplicates before
appending):
~~~bash
eval "$(zoxide init bash)"
eval "$(atuin init bash)"
eval "$(direnv hook bash)"
~~~
`direnv hook` must load after everything else in `.bashrc`, per direnv's
own documentation - place it last in the block.
</install>

<pasted_content id="2d1c">
Session jumping: switch the pick from tmux-sessionizer to sesh. Real finding, not a coin flip — t-smart-tmux-session-manager (the tool tmux-sessionizer competes with) is now explicitly superseded by its own author's rewrite, sesh (Go, not bash; multiplexer-agnostic; actively maintained where the older projects have stalled). (sesh discussion #230 (https://github.com/joshmedeski/sesh/discussions/230)) The decisive point: sesh natively integrates with zoxide — it treats your most-visited directories as session candidates automatically, no manual directory list to maintain. Since zoxide is getting installed anyway (next item), this isn't two separate tools bolted together, it's one coherent pick. tmux-sessionizer is a fine solo script, but sesh is the more actively maintained, more capable option doing the same job — install sesh, not tmux-sessionizer.

zoxide — confirmed, no real debate. autojump and fasd are both archived and unmaintained; zoxide is the unambiguous current standard, fastest, cross-shell. (zoxide.org (https://zoxide.org/comparisons/)) Install it.

Shell history: atuin, not mcfly — reversed from my earlier hesitation. Two concrete reasons this specific setup benefits from atuin over mcfly: it handles multi-line commands correctly where mcfly splits them (real, given how much multi-line/heredoc work AI agent sessions generate), and atuin's headline feature — encrypted, cross-machine history sync — directly matches a project you're already mid-build on ([[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap]]). mcfly's contextual ranking is genuinely clever but solves a smaller problem than sync does for you specifically. One correction to my earlier framing: this isn't a replacement for fzf, it's additive — atuin takes Ctrl-R for history, fzf keeps doing files/directories/completion, they don't conflict. (atuin.sh (https://atuin.sh/atuin-vs-fzf/))

direnv — confirmed, with a real caveat to build in from day one. Checked mise's own env-loading as the obvious competitor: mise and direnv are explicitly incompatible — "using direnv with mise is unsupported... PRs not accepted." Since nvm+uv are already your settled version managers (not mise), this isn't actually a live choice — direnv is the only option that doesn't reopen an already-closed decision. (mise.jdx.dev (https://mise.jdx.dev/direnv.html)) Real caveat found, worth configuring correctly from the start rather than fixing later: direnv stores whatever's in .envrc in plaintext — never put a real API key or token directly in one; source it from a separate, already-gitignored file instead. Same secrets discipline this whole rebuild already follows everywhere else.

gh-dash — confirmed, one alternative worth knowing about but not switching to. Found gitpane, a newer multi-repo dashboard with a lighter GitHub peek panel — but it's less mature and less specifically built around gh (which you already have authenticated). gh-dash stays the pick: most mature, largest adoption, and its lazygit hand-off is already confirmed real. (gh-dash.dev (https://www.gh-dash.dev/))

tmux agent-status plugin — the field is bigger than what got evaluated, and one candidate looks genuinely better. Beyond the original five, real search turned up more: tmux-agent-indicator, tmux-agent-signal (event-driven via Claude Code hooks, no polling), and tmux-handlr — which is the standout, because it's the only one that does something none of the others do: push notifications (via ntfy) when an agent finishes or needs input while you're not looking at the terminal. (CRThaze/tmux-handlr (https://github.com/CRThaze/tmux-handlr)) That's a direct, exact answer to the open question my own first research pass ended on. Whichever tmux-agent-status is currently installed solves in-terminal visibility; tmux-handlr solves the outside-the-terminal gap too. Worth checking its real commit activity before swapping (same "verify it's actually maintained" discipline as before) — but if it checks out, it's a genuine upgrade, not a lateral move.
</pasted_content id="2d1c">

## chafa
**What it is:** converts images to colored text-art so a terminal file manager can show a preview of a PNG/JPG without a graphics protocol.
**Confirmed working:** installed via apt (someone ran the command from last session's report). Tested standalone against a real PNG — renders genuine ANSI art from the actual image content, exit code 0.
**Crucial:** Yazi still shows a blank preview pane for that same image — confirmed again via raw escape-sequence inspection (zero image bytes emitted, same signature as before). Root cause now fully pinned down: Ubuntu 24.04's apt repo only ships chafa **1.14.0**, and Yazi's own docs require **≥1.16.0** before it will use chafa as a fallback at all. So installing chafa via apt was necessary but not sufficient — Yazi is silently refusing it on a version check. Confidence: **high** — verified chafa itself works, verified Yazi still doesn't invoke it, and the version gap matches Yazi's documented minimum exactly.
**Nice-to-have:** none beyond fixing the crucial item above (a newer chafa build — PPA, source, or static binary — since apt is capped at 1.14.0 on this Ubuntu release).

## zoxide
**What it is:** a smarter `cd` that learns your most-visited directories and jumps to them by partial name.
**Confirmed working:** installed (0.10.0). Real test: visited 4 real directories through separate interactive prompt cycles (had to drive this through tmux — a single non-interactive script doesn't trigger zoxide's per-prompt hook), then ran `z hermes` and `z agent-status` from `/` — both jumped to the correct directory. Added its `eval "$(zoxide init bash)"` hook to `.bashrc`.
**Crucial:** none.
**Nice-to-have:** none — works exactly as intended.

## sesh
**What it is:** a tmux session switcher that uses zoxide's visited-directories list as session candidates, so you don't maintain a separate project list.
**Confirmed working:** `sesh list` returns exactly zoxide's tracked directories (confirmed live integration, not just both-installed-separately). `~/.config/sesh/sesh.toml` does not exist yet — expected, real config is follow-up work.
**Crucial:** none for this session's scope.
**Nice-to-have:** a real `sesh.toml` (named sessions, startup commands per project) — deferred, as instructed.

## atuin
**What it is:** replaces bash's plain history with a searchable, richer local database (and optional encrypted sync, unused here).
**Confirmed working:** `atuin import auto` pulled in your real 230-line bash history. `atuin search TMUX_COPY_TEST` found the actual command from your earlier tmux clipboard test, correctly dated. `atuin status` returns "not logged in to a sync server" — confirmed local-only, no account created. One real gotcha caught: atuin's own installer had already appended its PATH/eval lines directly to the end of `.bashrc`, outside your `JARVIS_WSL_ENV_BLOCK` marker — adding the task's literal `eval "$(atuin init bash)"` a second time would have run it twice on every shell start. I left the installer's lines alone and only added zoxide's hook, so there's no duplication.
**Crucial:** none — works correctly as-is.
**Nice-to-have:** none noted; deeper config (key bindings, filter mode) is follow-up work.

## direnv
**What it is:** auto-loads/unloads a directory's `.envrc` environment variables when you `cd` in and out.
**Confirmed working:** **nothing — it isn't installed.** Same blocker as chafa last time: `sudo apt install -y direnv` needs a password I have no way to enter (`sudo -n -l` still returns "a password is required"). I deliberately did **not** add `eval "$(direnv hook bash)"` to `.bashrc` — that line would fail with "direnv: command not found" on every single shell start until direnv exists, which is exactly the kind of self-inflicted error you told me not to leave lying around.
**Crucial:** the tool doesn't exist yet, so the throwaway `.envrc` test couldn't be run. Confidence: **high** (verified blocker is identical to before, not a new mistake). Fix: run `sudo apt install -y direnv` yourself, then let me know and I'll add the hook line (which needs to load last in the block, per direnv's own docs).
**Nice-to-have:** n/a until installed.

## gh-dash
**What it is:** a terminal dashboard for your GitHub PRs, issues, and notifications, without leaving the terminal.
**Confirmed working:** ran it for real in a tmux pty (it needs an actual TTY, headless invocation fails outright) — it immediately showed your real open PR (`boom-astro/boom #364`). Worth correcting the task's assumption: it does **not** need to be "pointed at" specific repos for personal PR/issue tracking — the default sections use global GitHub search filters (`author:@me`, `review-requested:@me`) which work account-wide out of the box.
**Crucial:** none.
**Nice-to-have:** `repoPaths` (mapping repo names to local clone paths, so it can jump straight into your local checkout) is empty — genuine follow-up. Also, `pager.diff` in its config is blank — wiring it to `delta` (already installed and already used by git/lazygit) would make it consistent with the rest of the setup. Confidence: medium — real but purely cosmetic/convenience.

## tmux-agent-status vs. tmux-handlr (investigation)
Confirmed via git remote that the installed plugin is `samleeney/tmux-agent-status` (not the other repo sharing that name). Compared both for real:

| | samleeney/tmux-agent-status | CRThaze/tmux-handlr |
|---|---|---|
| Stars | 281 | 0 |
| Repo age | 14 months | 12 days |
| Last push | 9 days ago | 4 days ago |
| Recent commits | Real fixes/features | Real fixes/features |
| Closed issues | 4 (0 open) | 1 (0 open) |

Checked samleeney's own README to confirm the claimed gap is real: it has local "Notification Sounds" but nothing that reaches you when you're away from the terminal. tmux-handlr's push notifications (via ntfy) genuinely fill that gap, and its commit history (5 substantive commits in the 2 days before this session) shows real active development, not an abandoned scaffold.

**Decision: installed it alongside the existing plugin** (not a replacement). Verified clean load on a fresh tmux server — no errors, no extra panes spawned, `prefix+a` (agent switcher) and `prefix+A` (dashboard) both registered and work zero-config. Its push-notification feature specifically needs an ntfy topic configured, which is real follow-up config, not done here.
**Caveat worth flagging plainly:** 0 stars and 12 days old is a real risk signal — no one else has vetted this yet. The commit activity looks genuine, but this is a bet on a single-maintainer, unproven project, not a settled choice the way samleeney's plugin now is. Confidence: **medium** — worth keeping both for now, but revisit in a month and drop it if it goes quiet.

  echo "=== $repo ==="
  curl -s "https://api.github.com/repos/$repo" | python3 -c "
import json,sys
d = json.load(sys.stdin)
print('stars:', d.get('stargazers_count'), '| open_issues:', d.get('open_issues_count'), '| created:', d.get('created_at'), '| pushed:', d.get('pushed_at'), '| archived:', d.get('archived'))
"
  echo "--- recent commits ---"
  curl -s "https://api.github.com/repos/$repo/commits?per_page=5" | python3 -c "
import json,sys
d = json.load(sys.stdin)
if isinstance(d, dict):
    print('error:', d.get('message'))
else:
    for c in d:
        print(c['commit']['author']['date'], '-', c['commit']['message'].splitlines()[0])
"
  echo "--- closed issues ---"
  curl -s "https://api.github.com/search/issues?q=repo:$repo+type:issue+state:closed" | python3 -c "import json,sys; print('closed:', json.load(sys.stdin).get('total_count'))"
  echo
done

## Claude

That's a leftover from the `starship config` command I ran early in the first session — it opens an interactive editor and hangs when there's no terminal to attach to, so it got orphaned when that session ended. It's harmless: I abandoned that approach at the time and validated the config a different way (parsing it directly and rendering test prompts), so nothing was waiting on it. No action needed — everything from both sessions is already complete and reported above.

<fix name="chafa">
Root cause, confirmed by the previous session: Ubuntu 24.04's apt package
is capped at 1.14.0, and Yazi requires >=1.16.0 before it will use chafa
as an image-preview fallback at all - installing via apt was necessary
but not sufficient. Fix with the official static binary, not a PPA (the
only available PPA only reaches 1.14.5, still too old):

~~~bash
curl -fsSL -o /tmp/chafa-static.html https://hpjansson.org/chafa/releases/static/
grep -oP 'chafa-[\d.]+-sc\d+-x86_64-linux-gnu\.tar\.gz' /tmp/chafa-static.html | sort -V | tail -1
~~~

Use whatever exact filename that command actually prints (don't assume a
version number) to build the download URL
(`https://hpjansson.org/chafa/releases/static/<filename>`), download it,
extract the `chafa` binary, and install it to `~/.local/bin/chafa` - this
takes priority over the apt-installed 1.14.0 since `~/.local/bin` is
already first on `PATH` (the existing `JARVIS_WSL_ENV_BLOCK` in
`.bashrc`). Verify with `chafa --version` (must report >=1.16.0), then
open the same real image file from the previous test in Yazi inside a
real project repo and confirm an actual rendered preview now appears,
not a blank pane.
</fix>

<fix name="direnv">
Attempt the install again - if this session has real interactive sudo
access it may simply work this time:

~~~bash
sudo apt install -y direnv
~~~

Only if `command -v direnv` succeeds afterward, add
`eval "$(direnv hook bash)"` as the very last line of the
`JARVIS_WSL_ENV_BLOCK` in `.bashrc` (direnv's own docs require it to load
last), then verify with a throwaway test directory and a trivial
`.envrc` exactly as specified before, and delete the throwaway directory
afterward. If sudo is blocked again, do not add the hook line, and report
the exact same blocker plainly - don't leave a broken `eval` in `.bashrc`
under any circumstance.
</fix>

<fix name="atuin">
Add a `[keymap.emacs]`-adjacent settings pair to
`~/.config/atuin/config.toml` (create the file if it only has defaults so
far): `filter_mode = "global"` (Ctrl-R searches all history) and
`filter_mode_shell_up_key_binding = "directory"` (the Up arrow searches
only the current directory's history). This is atuin's own documented
recommended split, not an arbitrary choice. Verify by running a command
in one project directory, `cd`-ing to a different one, and confirming Up
only recalls history from the current directory while Ctrl-R still finds
the earlier one.
</fix>

<fix name="sesh">
Create `~/.config/sesh/sesh.toml`. Keep this structural only - no
`startup_command` that auto-runs anything on session creation, matching
the standing preference for manual over automatic tooling already
followed everywhere else in this environment (tmux itself doesn't
auto-start either). Just give it sensible defaults: a `default_session`
block, and confirm `sesh list`/`sesh connect` still work correctly against
real zoxide-tracked directories after the file exists (a malformed toml
would silently break the zoxide integration that already works - verify
it still works after adding the file, don't just assume the syntax is
right).
</fix>

<fix name="gh-dash">
Discover the real local repo -> GitHub remote mapping instead of guessing
names - for every git repo actually under `~/projects`:

~~~bash
find ~/projects -maxdepth 4 -type d -name .git | while read -r gitdir; do
  repo_dir=$(dirname "$gitdir")
  remote=$(git -C "$repo_dir" remote get-url origin 2>/dev/null)
  printf '%s\t%s\n' "$remote" "$repo_dir"
done
~~~

Use that real, live output (not an assumed repo list) to fill in
`repoPaths` in `~/.config/gh-dash/config.yml`, mapping each real
`owner/repo` -> its actual local path so gh-dash can jump straight into
the right checkout. Also set `pager.diff` to `delta` (already installed
and already used by git and lazygit - keeps every diff view in this
environment consistent). Verify by opening `gh dash`, picking the repo
matching the real open PR the previous session found
(`boom-astro/boom#364`), and confirming it opens the correct local
checkout.
</fix>

<fix name="tmux-handlr">
Wire up the ntfy push notification, `tmux-handlr`'s one real
differentiator over the already-installed `samleeney/tmux-agent-status`.
Set `@handlr-notify-command` in `.tmux.conf` to the plugin's own
ready-made pusher script
(`~/.tmux/plugins/tmux-handlr/scripts/agent-ntfy-notify.sh`, confirmed to
exist in the plugin's own repo - check it's actually there after TPM
installed the plugin before wiring it in). Generate one random,
hard-to-guess ntfy topic name (not a guessable word) and set it wherever
that script expects it (check the script's own header/env var for the
exact variable name - don't invent one). This uses the public ntfy.sh
server, not a self-hosted one - no server-side install needed. Do not
try to test receiving the notification yourself (that needs a phone with
the ntfy app subscribed to the same topic, which is the user's own
follow-up, not something this session can verify) - just confirm the
tmux option is set correctly and the script runs without erroring when
triggered manually once. Report the exact topic name clearly at the end
so the human can subscribe to it.
</fix>

<report>
End with a short status line per tool: fixed-and-verified,
fixed-but-needs-a-human-step (name the step), or still-blocked (name the
exact blocker, matching the direnv precedent - never silently drop a

I confirmed against tmux's official CHANGES file that `input-buffer-size` was only added in **tmux 3.6** — this system has **3.4**. total 28
drwxr-xr-x  2 anant_gupta anant_gupta  4096 Sep 19 01:18 .
drwxr-x--x 10 anant_gupta anant_gupta  4096 Sep 19 01:54 ..
-rw-r--r--  1 anant_gupta anant_gupta   325 Sep 19 01:18 atuin-receipt.json
-rw-------  1 anant_gupta anant_gupta 16094 Sep 19 01:18 config.toml
## Base directory for Atuin data files (databases, keys, session, etc.)
## All data file paths default to being relative to this directory.
## linux/mac: ~/.local/share/atuin (or XDG_DATA_HOME/atuin)
## windows: %USERPROFILE%/.local/share/atuin
# data_dir = "~/.local/share/atuin"

## where to store your database, default is your system data directory
## linux/mac: ~/.local/share/atuin/history.db
## windows: %USERPROFILE%/.local/share/atuin/history.db
# db_path = "~/.history.db"

## where to store your encryption key, default is your system data directory
## linux/mac: ~/.local/share/atuin/key
## windows: %USERPROFILE%/.local/share/atuin/key
# key_path = "~/.key"

## date format used, either "us" or "uk"
# dialect = "us"

## default timezone to use when displaying time
## either "l", "local" to use the system's current local timezone, or an offset
## from UTC in the format of "<+|->H[H][:M[M][:S[S]]]"
## for example: "+9", "-05", "+03:30", "-01:23:45", etc.
# timezone = "local"

## enable or disable automatic sync
# auto_sync = true

## enable or disable automatic update checks
# update_check = true

## which release channel to follow with `atuin update` and update checks
## "stable" for stable releases only, "nightly" to also receive prereleases
# update_channel = "stable"

## address of the sync server
# sync_address = "https://api.atuin.sh"

## how often to sync history. note that this is only triggered when a command
## is ran, so sync intervals may well be longer
## set it to 0 to sync after every command
# sync_frequency = "5m"

## which search mode to use for interactive search
## possible values: prefix, fulltext, fuzzy, daemon-fuzzy
# search_mode = "fuzzy"

## which search mode to use when atuin is invoked from a shell up-key binding
## the accepted values are identical to those of "search_mode"
## leave unspecified to use same mode set in "search_mode"
# search_mode_shell_up_key_binding = "fuzzy"

## which filter mode to use for interactive search
## possible values: "global", "host", "session", "session-preload", "directory", "workspace"
## consider using search.filters to customize the enablement and order of filter modes
# filter_mode = "global"

## which filter mode to use when atuin is invoked from a shell up-key binding
## the accepted values are identical to those of "filter_mode"
## leave unspecified to use same mode set in "filter_mode"
# filter_mode_shell_up_key_binding = "global"

## With workspace filtering enabled, Atuin will filter for commands executed
## in any directory within a git repository tree (default: false).
##
## To use workspace mode by default when available, set this to true and
## set filter_mode to "workspace" or leave it unspecified and
## set search.filters to include "workspace" before other filter modes.
# workspaces = false

## which display style to use
## possible values: auto, full, compact
# style = "compact"

## the maximum number of lines the interface should take up
## set it to 0 to always go full screen
# inline_height = 40

## the maximum number of lines the interface should take up
## when atuin is invoked from a shell up-key binding
## the accepted values are identical to those of "inline_height"
# inline_height_shell_up_key_binding = 0

## Invert the UI - put the search bar at the top , Default to `false`
# invert = false

## enable or disable showing a preview of the selected command
## useful when the command is longer than the terminal width and is cut off
# show_preview = true

## what to do when the escape key is pressed when searching
## possible values: return-original, return-query
# exit_mode = "return-original"

## possible values: emacs, subl
# word_jump_mode = "emacs"

## characters that count as a part of a word
# word_chars = "[REDACTED]"

## number of context lines to show when scrolling by pages
# scroll_context_lines = 1

## use ctrl instead of alt as the shortcut modifier key for numerical UI shortcuts
## alt-0 .. alt-9
# ctrl_n_shortcuts = false

## Show numeric shortcuts (1..9) beside list items in the TUI
## set to false to hide the moving numbers if you find them distracting
# show_numeric_shortcuts = true

## default history list format - can also be specified with the --format arg
# history_format = "{time}\t{command}\t{duration}"

## Defaults to true. If enabled, strip trailing spaces and tabs from commands
## before saving them to history.
## Escaped trailing spaces (for example `printf foo\\ `) are preserved.
# strip_trailing_whitespace = true

## prevent commands matching any of these regexes from being written to history.
## Note that these regular expressions are unanchored, i.e. if they don't start
## with ^ or end with $, they'll match anywhere in the command.
## For details on the supported regular expression syntax, see
## https://docs.rs/regex/latest/regex/#syntax
# history_filter = [
#   "^secret-cmd",
#   "^innocuous-cmd .*--secret=.+",
# ]

## prevent commands run with cwd matching any of these regexes from being written
## to history. Note that these regular expressions are unanchored, i.e. if they don't
## start with ^ or end with $, they'll match anywhere in CWD.
## For details on the supported regular expression syntax, see
## https://docs.rs/regex/latest/regex/#syntax
# cwd_filter = [
#   "^/very/secret/area",
# ]

## Configure the maximum height of the preview to show.
## Useful when you have long scripts in your history that you want to distinguish
## by more than the first few lines.
# max_preview_height = 4

## Configure whether or not to show the help row, which includes the current Atuin
## version (and whether an update is available), a keymap hint, and the total
## amount of commands in your history.
# show_help = true

## Configure whether or not to show tabs for search and inspect
# show_tabs = true

## Configure whether or not the tabs row may be auto-hidden, which includes the current Atuin
## tab, such as Search or Inspector, and other tabs you may wish to see. This will
## only be hidden if there are fewer than this count of lines available, and does not affect the use
## of keyboard shortcuts to switch tab. 0 to never auto-hide, default is 8 (lines).
## This is ignored except in `compact` mode.
# auto_hide_height = 8

## Defaults to true. This matches history against a set of default regex, and will not save it if we get a match. Defaults include
## 1. AWS key id
## 2. Github pat (old and new)
## 3. Slack oauth tokens (bot, user)
## 4. Slack webhooks
## 5. Stripe live/test keys
# secrets_filter = true

## If enabled, upon hitting enter Atuin will immediately execute the command,
## whereas tab will put the command in the prompt for editing.
## If set to false, both enter and tab will place the command in the prompt for editing.
## This applies for new installs. Old installs will keep the old behaviour unless configured otherwise.
enter_accept = true

## Defaults to false. If enabled, when triggered after &&, || or |, Atuin will complete commands to chain rather than replace the current line.
# command_chaining = false

## This specifies the keymap on the startup of `atuin search`. If this is set
## to "auto", the startup keymap mode in the Atuin search is automatically
## selected based on the shell's keymap where the keybinding is defined. If
## this is set to "emacs", "vim-insert", or "vim-normal", the startup keymap
## mode in the Atuin search is forced to be the specified one.
# keymap_mode = "emacs"

## Cursor style in each keymap mode. If specified, the cursor style is changed
## in entering the cursor shape. Available values are "default" and
## "{blink,steady}-{block,underline,bar}".
# keymap_cursor = { emacs = "blink-block", vim_insert = "blink-block", vim_normal = "steady-block" }

# network_connect_timeout = 5
# network_timeout = 30

## Extra HTTP headers to send on every request to the sync server. Useful when
## a self-hosted server sits behind a proxy that requires its own auth header,
## e.g. Cloudflare Access. Headers that Atuin sets itself (e.g. Authorization)
## cannot be overridden. When set, cross-origin redirects are refused so these
## headers are never sent to a different origin.
# extra_headers = { "CF-Access-Client-Id" = "...", "CF-Access-Client-Secret" = "..." }

## Timeout (in seconds) for acquiring a local database connection (sqlite)
# local_timeout = 2

## Set this to true and Atuin will minimize motion in the UI - timers will not update live, etc.
## Alternatively, set env NO_MOTION=true
# prefers_reduced_motion = false

## Defaults to true. Configures whether to store commands that failed (those with non-zero exit status) or not.
# store_failed = true

[stats]
## Set commands where we should consider the subcommand for statistics. Eg, kubectl get vs just kubectl
# common_subcommands = [
#   "apt",
#   "cargo",
#   "composer",
#   "dnf",
#   "docker",
#   "dotnet",
#   "git",
#   "go",
#   "ip",
#   "jj",
#   "kubectl",
#   "nix",
#   "nmcli",
#   "npm",
#   "pecl",
#   "pnpm",
#   "podman",
#   "port",
#   "systemctl",
#   "tmux",
#   "yarn",
# ]

## Set commands that should be totally stripped and ignored from stats
# common_prefix = ["sudo"]

## Set commands that will be completely ignored from stats
# ignored_commands = [
#   "cd",
#   "ls",
#   "vi"
# ]

[keys]
# Defaults to true. If disabled, using the up/down key won't exit the TUI when scrolled past the first/last entry.
# scroll_exits = true

# Defaults to true. The left arrow key will exit the TUI when scrolling before the first character
# exit_past_line_start = true

# Defaults to true. The right arrow key performs the same functionality as Tab and copies the selected line to the command line to be modified.
# accept_past_line_end = true

# Defaults to false. The left arrow key performs the same functionality as Tab and copies the selected line to the command line to be modified.
# accept_past_line_start = false

# Defaults to false. The backspace key performs the same functionality as Tab and copies the selected line to the command line to be modified when at the start of the line.
# accept_with_backspace = false

[preview]
## which preview strategy to use to calculate the preview height (respects max_preview_height).
## possible values: auto, static
## auto: length of the selected command.
## static: length of the longest command stored in the history.
## fixed: use max_preview_height as fixed height.
# strategy = "auto"

[daemon]
## Enables using the daemon to sync.
# enabled = false

## Automatically start and manage the daemon when needed.
## Not compatible with `systemd_socket = true`.
# autostart = false

## How often the daemon should sync in seconds
# sync_frequency = 300

## The path to the unix socket used by the daemon (on unix systems)
## linux/mac default:
## - if systemd_socket is false: $TMPDIR/atuin-$UID/atuin.sock
## - if systemd_socket is true: $XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/atuin.sock
## windows: Not Supported
# socket_path = "/custom/path/to/atuin.sock"

## The daemon pidfile used for lifecycle management.
## Defaults to the Atuin data directory.
# pidfile_path = "~/.local/share/atuin/atuin-daemon.pid"

## Use systemd socket activation rather than opening the given path (the path must still be correct).
## Note: setting this to true changes the default value of socket_path; see above.
## linux: false
## mac/windows: Not Supported
# systemd_socket = false

## The port that should be used for TCP on non unix systems
# tcp_port = 8889

# [pty_proxy]
## If enabled, `atuin init` will first re-exec your shell inside `atuin pty-proxy`,
## so you don't need a separate `eval "$(atuin pty-proxy init)"` line in your shell
## config. Supported for bash, zsh, fish and nu on unix platforms.
## For the fastest startup, keep your `atuin init` line near the top of your shell
## config: everything sourced before it runs again inside the proxy.
## Safe to combine with an existing `atuin pty-proxy init` line — the proxy only
## starts once.
# enabled = false

# [theme]
## Color theme to use for rendering in the terminal.
## There are some built-in themes, including the base theme ("default"),
## "autumn" and "marine". You can add your own themes to the "./themes" subdirectory of your
## Atuin config (or ATUIN_THEME_DIR, if provided) as TOML files whose keys should be one or
## more of AlertInfo, AlertWarn, AlertError, Annotation, Base, Guidance, Important, and
## the string values as lowercase entries from this list:
##    https://ogeon.github.io/docs/palette/master/palette/named/index.html
## If you provide a custom theme file, it should be  called "NAME.toml" and the theme below
## should be the stem, i.e. `theme = "NAME"` for your chosen NAME.
# name = "autumn"

## Whether the theme manager should output normal or extra information to help fix themes.
## Boolean, true or false. If unset, left up to the theme manager.
# debug = true

[search]
## The list of enabled filter modes, in order of priority.
## The "workspace" mode is skipped when not in a workspace or workspaces = false.
## Default filter mode can be overridden with the filter_mode setting.
# filters = ["global", "host", "session", "workspace", "directory", "session-preload"]

## Filter interactive search results by the shell used to run the command:
##
## - "all": show all commands
##
## - "auto": show commands run by the current type of shell, or commands that
##   have no shell recorded (for example, from before Atuin started tracking it)
##
## - array of strings: show commands run by any shell in the array; "" includes
##   commands that have no recorded shell
# shells = "auto"

[tmux]
## Enable using atuin with tmux popup (requires tmux >= 3.2)
## When enabled and running inside tmux, Atuin will use a popup window for interactive search.
## Set to false to disable the popup.
## This can also be controlled with the ATUIN_TMUX_POPUP environment variable.
## Note: The tmux popup is currently supported in zsh, bash, and fish shells. This currently doesn't work with iTerm native tmux integration.
# enabled = false

## Width of the tmux popup window
## Can be a percentage, or integer (e.g. "100" means 100 characters wide)
# width = "80%"

## Height of the tmux popup window
## Can be a percentage, or integer (e.g. "100" means 100 lines tall)
# height = "60%"

[ui]
## Columns to display in the interactive search, from left to right.
## The selection indicator (" > ") is always shown first implicitly.
##
## Each column can be specified as a simple string (uses default width)
## or as an object with type, width, and expand:
##   { type = "directory", width = 30, expand = true }
##
## Available column types (with default widths):
##   duration  (5)  - Command execution duration (e.g., "123ms")
##   time      (8)  - Relative time since execution (e.g., "59m ago")
##   datetime  (16) - Absolute timestamp (e.g., "2025-01-22 14:35")
##   directory (20) - Working directory (truncated if too long)
##   host      (15) - Hostname where command was run
##   user      (10) - Username
##   exit      (3)  - Exit code (colored by success/failure)
##   command   (*)  - The command itself (expands by default)
##
## The "expand" option (default: true for command, false for others) makes a
## column fill remaining space. Only one column should have expand = true.
##
## Default:
# columns = ["duration", "time", "command"]
##
## Examples:
##
## Minimal - more space for commands:
# columns = ["duration", "command"]
##
## With wider directory column:
# columns = ["duration", { type = "directory", width = 30 }, "command"]
##
## Show host for multi-machine sync users:
# columns = ["duration", "time", "host", "command"]
##
## Show exit codes prominently:
# columns = ["exit", "duration", "command"]
##
## Make directory expand instead of command:
# columns = ["duration", "time", { type = "directory", expand = true }, { type = "command", expand = false }]

## Syntax highlight commands in the search results, using the theme's colors.
## Not available on all platforms (e.g. Windows).
# syntax_highlight = true
  ```

  ```
  Sesh is a smart terminal session manager that helps you create and manage tmux sessions quickly and easily using      
  zoxide.                                                                                                               
         
  USAGE  
         
    sesh [command] [--flags]  
            
  COMMANDS  
            
    cache [command]                                Manage the session list cache
    clone <repo> [-- <extra git flags>] [--flags]  Clone a git repo and connect to it as a session
    completion [command]                           Generate the autocompletion script for the specified shell
    connect [--flags]                              Connect to the given session
    help [command]                                 Help about any command
    last                                           Connect to the last tmux session
    list [--flags]                                 List sessions
    mkdir <path> [--flags]                         Create a directory and connect to it as a session
    picker [--flags]                               Interactive session picker
    preview [--flags]                              Preview a session or directory
---config docs/example---
29:- **Session configuration** - define startup commands, windows, and preview commands per project in `sesh.toml`
30-- **Wildcard configs** - apply settings to all projects matching a glob pattern
31-- **Built-in picker** - interactive session selector, or integrate with fzf, television, or gum
32-- **Clone and connect** - clone a git repo and start a session in one step
33-- **Mkdir and connect** - create a new directory (relative or absolute path) and start a session in one step
34-- **Last session switching** - seamlessly bounce between your two most recent sessions
35-- **Root session navigation** - jump to the root of a git worktree or repository
36-- **Nerd Font icons** - display session type icons in your picker
37-- **Shell completions** - tab completion for Bash, Zsh, Fish, and PowerShell

586:You can configure sesh by creating a `sesh.toml` file in your `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/sesh` or `$HOME/.config/sesh` directory.
587-
588-```sh
589:mkdir -p ~/.config/sesh && touch ~/.config/sesh/sesh.toml
590-```
591-
592-### Custom Config Path
593-
594-You can specify a custom config file path using the `--config` (or `-C`) flag. This is useful for NixOS wrappers, maintaining separate work/private configurations, or testing.
595-
596-```sh
597:sesh -C /path/to/custom/sesh.toml list
598:sesh --config /path/to/custom/sesh.toml connect my-session
599-```
600-
601-The flag works with any subcommand. When specified, the file must exist or sesh will return an error. Without the flag, sesh uses the default config path.
602-
603-### Custom Multiplexer (psmux)
604-
605:Sesh uses `tmux` as the default terminal multiplexer, but you can configure it to use any tmux-compatible multiplexer like [psmux](https://github.com/psmux/psmux) by setting `tmux_command` in your `sesh.toml`:
606-
607-```toml
608-tmux_command = "psmux"
609-```
610-
611-This replaces the `tmux` binary in all commands sesh runs (session creation, switching, attaching, etc.). The configured multiplexer must support tmux's CLI interface.
612-
613-### Custom Frecency Backend (fasd, autojump, etc.)
  ```

---
title: Repo Paths
---

You can use the `repoPaths` setting to map repository names (as keys) to local paths
(as values). This map configures where to locate repositories when checking out PRs.

The mappings can be exact matches, like the full name or path of a repository, or wildcard
matches using the `owner` and a partial path.

An exact match for the full repository name to a full path takes priority over a matching
wildcard. Wildcard keys must match to a wildcard path.

The `RepoName` and `RepoPath` keybinding arguments are fully expanded when sent to the
command.

## Examples

### Matching Repositories

```yaml
repoPaths:
  dlvhdr/*: ~/code/repos/*
  dlvhdr/gh-dash: ~/code/gh-dash
```

In this example, the first key is defined with a wildcard (`*`) as `dlvhdr/*`, so the
dashboard will use this entry to resolve the path for any repo in the `dlvhdr`
namespace, like `dlvhdr/jb` or `dlvhdr/harbor`. Note that the value for this key
also uses a wildcard. If the key specifies a wildcard, the value must specify one too.

If a repository in the `dlvhdr` namespace has been cloned into the `~/code/repos`
folder, the dashboard will be able to checkout PRs for that repository locally.

If the repository isn't found, the checkout command raises an error.

The second key defines an exact mapping between the `dlvhdr/gh-dash` repository and the
`~/code/gh-dash` folder. Because this is a non-wildcard mapping, the dashboard will use
this value to resolve the repository path for `dlvhdr/gh-dash` even though there's also
an entry for `dlvhdr/*`.

## Pattern Properties

### With a Wildcard

If the key for an `repoPath` entry ends with a wildcard (`*`), its value must also have
a wildcard. If a key ends with a wildcard but the value doesn't, `gh-dash` won't be
able to correctly map repositories to folders.

### Without a Wildcard

If the key for an `repoPath` entry doesn't have a wildcard (`*`), its value must not
have a wildcard. If a key ends without a wildcard but the value does, `gh-dash` won't
be able to correctly map repositories to folders.
  ```
- `Edit` — `/home/anant_gupta/.config/gh-dash/config.yml`

  ```

#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

agent="${AGENT_NAME:-agent}"
state="${AGENT_STATE:-}"
sess="${AGENT_SESSION:-}"
win="${AGENT_WINDOW:-}"

# Only notify for the configured states (the daemon filters too; this is a second guard).
ntfy_states="${AGENT_NTFY_STATES:-done}"
case ",$ntfy_states," in
	*",$state,"*)
		;;
	*)
		exit 0
		;;
esac

env_file="${AGENT_NTFY_ENV:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/tmux-handlr/ntfy.env}"
[ -r "$env_file" ] || exit 0
set -a; . "$env_file"; set +a

url="${HANDLR_NTFY_URL:-${HERDR_NTFY_EXTERNAL_URL:-}}"
topic="${HANDLR_NTFY_TOPIC:-${HERDR_NTFY_TOPIC:-}}"
token="${HANDLR_NTFY_TOKEN:-${HERDR_NTFY_BEARER_TOKEN:-}}"
if [ -z "$url" ] || [ -z "$topic" ]
then
	exit 0
fi

auth=()
case "$token" in
	"")
		;;
	*:*)
		auth=(-u "$token")   # user:pass
		;;
	*)
		auth=(-H "Authorization: Bearer $token")   # bearer token
		;;
  ```

## Claude

Let me verify the tmux upgrade path for real before handing you commands — no point giving you something that turns out wrong.

Verified both paths for real before handing these over — no PPA on Ubuntu 24.04 reaches tmux ≥3.6 (checked the usual `pi-rho/dev` PPA; its newest build is from 2019), so tmux needs a source build. Here's the exact, verified command list:

**1. direnv** (the only piece still missing from last time — apt alone, sudo needed):
```bash
sudo apt install -y direnv
```

**2. tmux upgrade to 3.6+** (needed for Yazi's image previews — confirmed via tmux's own CHANGES file that `input-buffer-size` landed in 3.6; this box has 3.4, and no PPA reaches it, so it's a source build into `~/.local` — doesn't touch the apt package, keeps working with everything already configured):
```bash
sudo apt install -y libevent-dev libncurses-dev pkg-config
cd /tmp
curl -sLO https://github.com/tmux/tmux/releases/download/3.7c/tmux-3.7c.tar.gz
tar -xzf tmux-3.7c.tar.gz
cd tmux-3.7c
./configure --prefix="$HOME/.local"
make -j"$(nproc)"
make install
cd /
rm -rf /tmp/tmux-3.7c*
hash -r
tmux -V
```
That last line should print `tmux 3.7c` (confirming `~/.local/bin` is picking up over the apt one, same pattern as chafa). **Kill any running tmux server before you trust that** (`tmux kill-server`) — the old 3.4 server binary stays in memory until you do, even after the new one's on PATH.

**3. ntfy** — not a command, a phone-app step: install the ntfy app and subscribe to topic `[REDACTED]`.

Come back whenever — I'll verify the tmux version, re-test Yazi's image preview for real, add direnv's hook line, and run the throwaway `.envrc` test once these are in.

## Claude

Found it — this one's on me. `configure`'s log (`/tmp/tmux-3.7c/config.log`, still there since you didn't clean up) shows the real failure clearly:

```
configure:6100: checking for yacc
configro:6128: result: no
configure:6137: error: "yacc not found"
```

Everything else passed fine (libevent, ncurses, pkg-config all found correctly). I dropped `bison` from the dependency list earlier because I saw the tarball already ships a pre-generated `cmd-parse.c` and assumed that made a parser generator unnecessary — but tmux's `configure` script hard-checks for `yacc` unconditionally regardless of that, via the standard `AC_PROG_YACC` autoconf macro, and just fails if nothing (`bison`, `byacc`, or `yacc` itself) is on `PATH`. My mistake, not yours.

The fix is a single missing package. Your extracted source at `/tmp/tmux-3.7c` is still there and fine to reuse — no need to re-download:

```bash
sudo apt install -y bison
cd /tmp/tmux-3.7c
./configure --prefix="$HOME/.local"
make -j"$(nproc)"
make install
cd /
rm -rf /tmp/tmux-3.7c*
hash -r
tmux -V
```

That last line should print `tmux 3.7c`. I dug further and found yazi actually gets much further now — it successfully decodes and caches a resized copy of the image — but the final on-screen paint step still doesn't show anything, across 8 rapid consecutive captures with zero variation (so it's not a flicker I'm missing). I'll report this precisely rather than claim it's fully fixed. Moving to direnv now.

## 1. Technical fixes — final status

**tmux**: now 3.7c, confirmed via `tmux -V`, and every custom binding, plugin option, and the win32yank clipboard bridge were re-tested live and still work correctly after the version jump. Nothing broke.

**direnv**: fully installed and wired. The hook is the last line in `JARVIS_WSL_ENV_BLOCK`. Tested for real: a throwaway `.envrc` was blocked by default (correct security behavior), loaded after `direnv allow`, the variable was visible in the shell, and it unloaded cleanly on `cd` back out. The throwaway directory and its orphaned allow-list entry are both deleted.

**chafa image preview in Yazi**: honestly, still not rendering, even though both root causes I found are genuinely fixed. Here is exactly what I confirmed:
- Chafa itself: 1.18.2, confirmed rendering real images standalone.
- The tmux passthrough error from yazi's own log (`mux.rs:40`, missing `input-buffer-size`) is gone. Confirmed the pane-level `allow-passthrough` is now correctly set to `all` while yazi is running.
- Yazi's pipeline gets further than before: it successfully decodes the source PNG and writes a resized 1200x736 JPEG to its cache, which was not happening as a distinguishable step before.
- Even so, nothing appears on screen. I captured eight rapid frames a fraction of a second apart and they were byte-for-byte identical, so it is not a flicker I am missing between captures. Based on yazi's own source, the render is a raw terminal write with explicit cursor positioning, separate from its normal UI redraw. Something in that final step is not completing, and I cannot isolate it further through scripted captures. This needs a real interactive session with eyes on the screen, not another round of text-capture guessing from me.

I am not going to keep chasing this blind. It is a real, narrowed-down problem for a future session, not a silently broken thing I am pretending is fine.

## 3. `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`

Created fresh (it did not exist before). It now has two rules, exactly as you asked:

1. Never write files or artifacts anywhere on disk unless the user explicitly asked for that file in that session. Config edits and installs that were actually requested are fine; an unrequested extra note or log file is not.
2. Never use em dashes. Prefer full sentences with normal punctuation. Dashes only where grammar genuinely needs one, not as a stand in for a comma.

I have applied both rules in this reply.

## 4. What the WizTree images actually show

D drive is healthy: 75 GB used out of 636 GB, and the real WSL distro disk is correctly sitting there at `D:\WSL\Ubuntu\ext4.vhdx`, about 3.6 to 3.7 GB.

C drive has 99 GB used out of 315 GB. I went looking for the specific "WSL populates C" failure mode from your old laptop, and found a real, concrete cause, not a vague guess.

`C:\Program Files\WSL` at 827.9 MB is just the WSL platform itself (the Windows feature, not distro data). That is normal, expected, and cannot be moved. That one is fine.

The real issue: **Docker Desktop has its own separate WSL distro that is not on D.** I checked `wsl --list --verbose` directly and found a `docker-desktop` distro alongside your `Ubuntu-24.04`. Its disks live at:
```
C:\Users\anant\AppData\Local\Docker\wsl\disk\docker_data.vhdx
C:\Users\anant\AppData\Local\Docker\wsl\main\ext4.vhdx
```
Right now that folder is only 1.7 GB, so it is not a problem yet. But this is exactly the kind of thing that grows silently over time as you build images and run containers, entirely on C, while you believe everything WSL related is on D because you only relocated the Ubuntu distro. This is almost certainly the mechanism behind the old laptop's C drive filling up.

Also worth a look: `C:\Windows\LiveKernelReports` is holding 1.1 GB. That folder accumulates crash dump artifacts, and a WSL or Hyper V crash is one of the things that writes there.

Your `.wslconfig` already exists and already points `swapfile` at `D:\WSL\swap.vhdx`, which is good. It has no explicit disk size cap set for either distro.

  periodic `Optimize-VHD`) so that neither the Ubuntu distro nor Docker's

MARKER="FINAL_VERIFY_$(date +%s)"
tmux send-keys -t final-smoke "clear; printf '%s\n' $MARKER" Enter
sleep 0.3
tmux copy-mode -t final-smoke
tmux send-keys -t final-smoke -X history-top
sleep 0.1
tmux send-keys -t final-smoke -X search-forward "$MARKER"
sleep 0.2
tmux send-keys -t final-smoke V
sleep 0.1
tmux send-keys -t final-smoke y
sleep 0.3
READBACK=$(~/.local/bin/win32yank.exe -o)
echo "sent: $MARKER"
echo "got : $READBACK"
echo "$READBACK" | grep -q "$MARKER" && echo "WIN32YANK: STILL WORKS" || echo "WIN32YANK: BROKEN"
  ```
